Attachments: QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 5/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HG's Black Sheep Journal #203 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 I believe we can call the modifications set. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 6/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The new modifications are: A. There must be a WRB in the sequence. This isn't so much a new rule as it is making the appearance of a WRB definitive. B. The initial Start Point (SP) must appear 1-4 intervals after the WRB. We've gone from 1-3 to 1-4. Therefore, there can be 3 intervals after the WRB before we see the SP. C. The Long Lower Shadow (for longs) of the initial Start Point (SP) must be greater than the lower shadows of all the intervals after the WRB and the 3 intervals prior to the WRB. And the Long Lower Shadow must be larger than the bodies of said intervals as well. But the Long Lower Shadow does not have to be larger than the combination of the lower shadow and the body of any interval except its own. If we care then that bearish SP woul be invalid. I know that CC is missing in this picture but the question is about the rules for identifying the SP candles. Akif, Great questions, keep them coming. I will start with this one first. One of our goals is to create an entry signal that is specific and rule-based, yet broad enough to cover a myriad of situations. To that end, we have not mentioned any type of color (close vs open) requirements for any of the intervals. I have noticed that the majority (almost all) of the valid short signals will have a White (close>open) WRB. And the valid long signals will have Dark (close<open) WRB. But again, that is not a requirement, only an observation. Along those same lines, we have not made any such requirement for any of the intervals involved in the pattern. For example, we have NOT said that there must be a Dark (close>open) interval within the 3 intervals prior to the WRB. Once again, great question. I appreciate your participation. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 12/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #209 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 [quote="akifusenet" post=43700]Another trick question is: What is there are consecutive WRBs before the SP? which WRB's prev 3 candles will be looked at? FORUM HOME EDUCATION MORE LOGOUT No. The CC has two rules: (1) the CC must close above the body of the previous candle. (2) the body of the CC must be larger than the body of the previous candle. So our SP & CC combo must meet both rules of the CC and all the rules of the SP. This chart was taken from a previous post by akifusenet. I added the labels based on the new version of the Confirmation Entry. #2 is no longer the CC, it is our first (and in this case only) chance to get into the market. One thing I have avoided discussing is the idea of a "contingency" plan. What I mean by "contingency" plan is price action that would not only tell us we are wrong, but would also suggest we take a trade in the opposite direction. I do not like "contingency" plan set-ups, because they would have us going long in Supply Zones and short in Demand Zones. However, we may still want to focus on a particular Price Action piece typically found in a "contingency" plan. The WRB. I will get into the concept on the next post. For now, just realize that we also have a Nullification Candle (NC). The white (open<close) WRB in this picture, although we are not happy to see it, would not be a NC. One thing I should mention about placing our stops. I have been saying the stop should be roughly 5 pips from the high/low of the SP. I have been using 5 because that is what DTML use. But they are taking the trades on a 15 minute chart. 5 pips may or may not be appropriate on a 1 hour chart. They are also trying to keep the over risk to 20 pips. Again, 20 pips may or may not be appropriate on a 1 hour time frame. Akif, While there will always be losing trades, let's take a closer look at these here. A1: I am not sure that this is in fact a valid SP. The long lower shadow does not look larger than the body of the candle before it. Nor does it look larger than the lower shadow of the candle before it. The Confirmation Entry is a VISUAL ENTRY. That means the better set-ups tend to be those that you can tell meet the requirements visually with ease. Now, you may do the actual math and conclude that lower shadow is larger than the body of the previous candle. But when you can make that determination easily with the naked eye, the set-up tends to be better. B1: This is more of an obvious valid SP. A valid signal is produced, which results in a loss. Let's take a closer look at B5. This is a White (open<close) WRB that appears 1-3 intervals after our entry candle and closes higher than the high of the entry candle. That is a Nullification Candle (NC). In this case, the NC stops us out. But if it had not stopped us out, we would want to be exiting, or at the very least, moving our stop closer to our entry price. A true "contingency" plan would have us stop and reverse our position. Especially since the WRB preceded a doji candle line. The other place where an NC can appear is after the SP but before confirmation. This NC would tell us not to enter the trade at all. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 29/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Note that our NC could be the interval that both enters us into the trade during its creation and tells us to exit once it is complete. Which is the case here. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #221 Posts: 143 HiddenGap Offline Trader Thank you received: 186 Here's a schematic with a few examples of the NC. Key points: 30/38 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT 1. Any white (open<close) WRB that closes higher than the high of our SP is a NC. If the NC appears before the CC, we would not enter at all. 2. If we are in a short trade, a white (open<close) WRB 1-3 intervals after our entry candle that closes higher than the high of said candle, is a NC. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #3 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 A lot of well meaning people often make a huge mistake when trying to explain why the market did what it did at any given time. They say things like, " the market went up today because there were more buyers than sellers". Or they say," the market went down due to a 'seller's' strike". These are completely wrong. The market's sole purpose is to bring a buyer and a seller together. Every price where a transaction takes place MUST have a buyer and a seller. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 5/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community WHAT MOVES THE MARKETS, IS THE RELATIVE ENTHUSIASM OR AGGRESSION HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT OF EITHER THE BUYER(S) OR THE SELLER(S). Let's take a simplified example. Suppose you want to buy a car. The price you are willing to pay is 50,000. The seller of the car is asking for 100,000. Now let's suppose we can't just split the difference and make a transaction at 75,000. The car will sit there and no transaction will happen unless one of you is willing to move. Suppose you as a buyer see three more potential buyers coming. You may now have incentive to raise your price to 100,000. You are now willing to hit the ASK. This willingness is enthusiasm or aggression. The opposite could be true as well. Suppose the Seller becomes aware that another sellers of the same car is coming and is willing to take 45,000. The first seller would have incentive to quickly lower his price to 50,000. That is, the seller needs to hit the BID and make a transaction happen before the buyers gets word of the other car on the market. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 6/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT This is what actually moves the market. Buyers willingly hitting the ASK will raise price and Sellers willingly hitting the BID will cause price to fall. Therefore, If we want to determine where a key change in the supply/demand dynamic as taken place, we look for where the sellers have been more aggressive (hitting the BID), which we call supply. Or we look for where the buyers have been more aggressive (hitting the ASK), which we call demand. There are a few more things that go into the definition of an Aggression bar. The main point is that for every buyer there is a seller. A transaction takes place when one side moves aggressively to accept the price from the other. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #5 Posts: 143 HiddenGap Offline Trader 8/37 Thank you received: 186 So, how do we interpret aggression on a price chart? There are some basic elements we are looking at: (1) Volume https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community (2) Range EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME (3) Effort (4) MidPoint trend (5) Open/Close percentages (6) Hidden Gaps (7) Facilitation " Green (+ tick volume and + MFI) A Green signifies three things: 1. More players are entering the market (+ volume); 2. The arriving players are biased in the direction the bar is moving; 3. The price movement is picking up speed as it goes (+ MFI)." Bill Williams This is a key concept for understanding an aggression bar. We want to see a bar that has an increasing range on increasing volume. And we want to see volume proportional to the range. Another very key factor is "MidPoint trend". If the midpoint of the current bar is greater than or equal to the high of the previous bar, the immediate trend is UP. If the midpoint of the current bar is less than or equal to the low of the previous bar, the immediate trend is DOWN. If buyers are being more aggressive than sellers, then one would expect to see the midpoint trend to be UP. If sellers are being more aggressive than buyers, then one would expect to see the midpoint trend to be DOWN. We are also looking at volatility. We are measuring this in two ways. We are looking for candle bodies that are greater than the previous 3 intervals. Thus making the interval the largest candle body in 4 intervals (inclusive). This is the candle definition of a WRB, where B stands for body. We go one step further. We also look for the range to be the largest range (H-L) in 4 intervals (inclusive). This is the bar definition of a WRB, where B stands for bar. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 9/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT To determine if an aggression bar is a key change in the supply/demand dynamic in the market, we will need to look at the price action following it. But before we do that, there is one element of the subsequent price action we need to see to determine if a true aggression bar occurred. All aggression bars form Hidden Gaps. Take a look at the chart above. Notice that the low of the candle after the "Climber" is greater than the high of the candle prior to the "Climber". This area is a Hidden Gap. If we were to erase the middle interval we would have an actual gap. We know actual gaps are valid supply/demand delta zones. These Hidden Gaps may be signaling a valid zone as well. Posts: 463 Thank you received: 910 I've read about the hidden gap before, and it's a concept that has stayed with me, and popped back into my head when I encountered the PAZ (price action zone) here, and the assertion that should price enter the PAZ, it shall move to the other side. I think, from memory, that is the case for a hidden gap, too? I've marked a couple of RTM terms for the two edges of the HG, here. Point "1" is the decision for price to move higher, and the subsequent candle forms a pole up. Should price continue up from the current candle, then "2" shall become an FTR, and also become a flag limit. If that FTR/FL is broken to the downside (price enters the hidden gap) then the RTMers would anticipate a move down to the base of the pole. This would also, of course, fill the hidden gap. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #16 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 My last post to Romano83 got me thinking. Actually, it brought up an issue that I have been struggling with for a long time: what is the best "Entry Signal Method" to use. In trading as in life, there are trade offs. No one method is perfect all the time. Supply/Demand Zone trading seems to offer a unique situation with respect to entries. As I have stated before, I am not a "limit order" proponent. The main reason being I do not believe that a Supply or a Demand Zone is a trade signal in and of itself. I also believe this to be the case with Support and Resistance, which are not the same things as Supply and Demand. Now I know that a the market can't always respect a zone (supply or demand), but it is the respecting of these zones that tends to give belief and trust in them. Yet, suppose the market respected the zones 90% of the time. However each time it did, it failed to give an entry signal. Where's the profit in that? There are many times when the market respects my Supply/Demand Zones and no trade signal is given. This begs the question are the entry signal patterns lacking? Do I https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 20/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community need less strict patters or more patterns in general? Although there are VSA patterns, I HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT look at VSA as the alternative to a price pattern based entry signal. On the surface, VSA would seem to make more sense with Aggression Bars. This is not a VSA thread, so I do not want to get too much into it. This is an example from Friday of the Yen. Point A. is a wide spread down interval on increasing volume. However, note that the volume is less than average. The volume is also disproportionally small compared to the range of the interval. Of course note that this down interval is into an area where the buyers were previously aggressive. Point B. is an Squat. The range of this interval is small than the previous interval, but the volume is greater. And although the interval makes a lower low than the previous interval it actually closes on its high. From a VSA perspective, this can only mean buying. This is a possible entry point. It's a bit "aggressive", but it tells the story you https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 21/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community would want to hear. In an area where buyers were previously aggressive, we see HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT increased activity( volume=activity) on an interval that is closing on the high after a wide spread down interval that was lacking in activity. Point C. This is your basic No Supply. This down interval has volume less than the previous two intervals. In fact, it is colored grey because it is the lowest volume in the last 20 intervals. Volume is activity of the professional trader. Therefore if volume is this low, we can conclude that the professional traders are not interested in lower prices. If they are not interested in lower prices, it must be because they expect prices to rise. Again, buyers have already demonstrated that they were the more aggressive ones in area of the price chart. It should be of no surprise that as price falls back into this area the sellers are not aggressive and are in fact withdrawing. Anyway, Point B is not a true hammer so I don't have a valid Hammer pattern for entry. None of the other patterns I look for are there either. So if I use VSA, I can get into this trade. If not, I can only take satisfaction in seeing the Supply/Demand Zone respected. Just seeing the Zone(s) respected is nice, but leaves your account the same. And lest I forget, there are certainly times when the VSA does not give an entry signal but a price pattern appears. It's late and I am just rambling now. Forgive me. I would like to hear from some others who went through this struggle and how they came to reconcile it. Tony Robbins QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #18 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 23/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 Once we have an Aggression bar that forms a hidden gap, we know we have a change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic in the market. However, not all changes are key changes. Only key changes in the Supply/Demand Dynamic become our Supply/Demand Zones. In order to determine if a key change has occurred, we want to look at the price action following the appearance of the Aggression Bar. What we want to see is strong continuation. There are a couple of definitions of strong continuation. below is an example of one. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 24/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Notice that after the Aggression interval we see two consecutive white (close>open) that are making higher highs and not lower lows and are closing higher. Also not that both close higher than the close of the Aggression bar. Basically, we are defining what Sam asks when he asks, "how does the market leave the (would-be) zone"? Attachments: 25/37 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community The following user(s) said Thank You: Springger, changbachang, fxtiger, fxyogi, cuimingzhao HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #19 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 Another example of the type of price action we want to see to confirm a key change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic has taken place involves Fractals. Fractal highs or lows are reaction points. They are also decision points. If after the appearance of an Aggression bar, we see price move back into the range of the bar but fails to close the Hidden Gap before moving back out and forms a fractal, the market is telling us that this is in fact a Key change in the market. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 26/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Closing the Hidden Gap is very important. We never want to see price action close the HG prior to meeting a definiton that tells us a zone is created. Once the zone is created, the HG portion does not matter. A zone is open until price shows conviction above or below the zone.......... Does that now void this zone for you for any future trading? Hello my friend. The simple answer is no. The Hidden Gap alerts us that there has been a change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic in the market. Once the market has shown that the change was KEY (via strong continuation for example) a Supply/Demand Zone is created. At this time, the Hidden Gap portion of the zone does not really matter much. Trade signals are valid as long as the Zone remains open. There are some traders who only use the Hidden Gap as the Zone. If that were the case, this zone would still be valid for future trading because the interval that closed the Hidden Gap is involved in a valid trading pattern. The following user(s) said Thank You: changbachang, cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 33/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community HG's Black Sheep Journal #25 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MHOiRdEdenGaLpOGOUT Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 Here's a good example of what the Hidden Gap can be telling us. Notice in this example that we have a Drifter (interval where the aggression is by the sellers) that forms a hidden gap. We don't know if we have a KEY change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic, but we know we have had a change in it. We also note that the recent fractal low is within the range of https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal 34/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - RTM Community the Hidden Gap. If the fractal low is some HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT sort of decision point, then for this interval to be "gapping" through it must be of some importance. Still, we don't know for sure until we see the subsequent price action. In this case we do get strong continuation. The definition of strong continuation in this case is, three dark (close<open) intervals with lower highs and lower lows closing lower than the close of the Aggression bar prior to then next swing. Where one of the three dark intervals is an Aggression bar that forms a Hidden Gap. At the bottom we see an valid "fakie" long trade signal. It closes the gap but it does not close the zone. I like to see conviction to close a zone. Conviction would be a full (non doji) candle body below the zone, where said body's interval is not involved in a valid trade signal pattern. This appears to be a nice example of the signal. However, there is a reason not to take this signal long. We have already established that we only need 1 pip/tick of one interval of the signal in or on a Supply/Demand Delta Zone for a long. Based on that, would we want to take a long trade if 1pip/tick of the signal is in or on a Supply/Demand Delta Zone? No. So this set- up would be disregarded because at least one pip of one interval is in the wrong place. The Confirmation Short signal basically sets up entirely within a Supply/Demand Delta Zone. That does not mean price can't go down of course. It just means if it is going https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=225 2/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 10 - RTM Community down, it's going down without us. As we prefer to take Longs in Demand and Shorts in HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Supply. A picture is better than a thousand words so we will start with a picture. Whats below is for bullish, reverse for bearish. We start with searching for 3 consecutive bars conforming 1. Candle 2 "OR" 3 is a WRB 2. Candle 1 "AND" 2 is White (Open<Close) 3. Candle 1 has a higher high than candle 2 4. Candle 2 has a higher high than candle 3 5. Candle 1 has a higher close than candle 2 6. Candle 2 has a higher close than candle 3 If these rules are passed we found our candidate consecutive bar chain. Now we are looking to the left to find some fractal highs. Candle 2 and candle 3 are our candidates for closing higher than these fractal highs. We will check them separately. Candle 2 or candle3 must be the first candle reaching these fractal highs. (Meaning https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=225 10/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 10 - RTM Community there shouldn't be any candle which has a equal or higher high than the fractal high in HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT between the fractal and the our candle 2/3. Now the rules between fractal highs and our candle2 /candle3: 1. FH(meaning Fractal High) must be higher than Open of candle 2/3 2. FH must be lower than Close of candle 2/3 3. (For candle2) Open of candle2 must be lower than the low of candle1 4. (For candle3) Open of candle3 must be lower than the low of candle2 5. (For candle2) ZONE= High of candle2 and Low of candle1 6. (For candle3) ZONE= High of candle3 and Low of candle2 For now there isn't any constraint for the number of FHs to be closed over. Only one FH is enough for a zone to be valid. Besides being an entry signal, the CE can form the basis for a Supply/Demand Zone as well. When we see a signal that would be valid if it occurred on or within a Supply/Demand Zone, we can use the Demand Swamping Supply (DSS) or the Supply Swamping Demand (SSD) interval's long shadow to form a zone. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=225 34/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 10 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I think I prefer Supply Swamping Demand (SSD) and Demand Swamping Supply (DSS) to SP as SSD and DSS more acurately describe what the CE signal is all about. In addition, although some do like to identify the Long Shadow (SP) first and then work backwards, most would find it easier to first identify the WRB. The WRB interval acts like a notification that a CE entry signal may be forming. So in essence, the WRB might more accurately be thought of as the SP. A BreakOut WRB is defined as a WRB that closes higher or lower than the range of the previous three intervals. 2) For a valid long CE signal, the BreakOut WRB must be a dark interval (open>close). 3) For a valid short CE signal, the BreakOut WRB must be a white interval (open<close). This is really our "start point" as there is no signal without the appearance of the WRB. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=225 39/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 10 - RTM Community AttachHmOenMtsE: EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: Whenever QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #249 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 Quick note. (I will give the rules for a bullish signal, reverse the needed parts for a bearish signal) The DSS candle must: (1) have a low lower than the lows of the prior 3 intervals. (2) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle, must be larger than the combination of its Upper shadow and its body. (3) The DSS must appear 1-4 intervals after the appearance of a dark breakout WRB. Therefore, there can be 3 intervals after the dark breakout WRB before we see the DSS. (4) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than the lower shadows of all the intervals after the dark breakout WRB. (5) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than both the lower shadows and the bodies of the 3 intervals Prior to the dark breakout WRB. NMowORI rEeally LOGOUT wondered...Akif, I will get to that shortly. I wanted to jump ahead to the Confirmation Candle (CC). While we call this the CE pattern in part because it was already called that, we mainly call this the CE pattern because the Entry signal Confirms what the pattern signifies. Ultimately, our pattern is thought to represent a change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic in the market. At the heart of this change, is either the Demand Swamping Supply (DSS) inverval or the Supply Swamping Demand (SSD) interval. The CC : 1. For a long, the CC must close higher than the previous candle. And for a short, the CC must close lower than the previous candle. 2. The body (ABS(Open-Close)) of the CC must be larger than the body (ABS(Open- Close)) of the previous candle. Where, ABS stands for absolute value. 3. For a long, the close of the CC must be greater than the midpoint of the Long Shadow of the DSS interval. For a short, the close of the CC must be less than the midpoint of the Long Shadow of the SSD interval. Note: Based on the above rules, the CC can be the same interval as the SSD/DSS interval. 4. Progression. The maximum number of intervals that can appear after the SSD/DSS interval and the CC interval is three (3). Which is to say, if the SSD/DSS interval is not also the CC interval, the CC interval must occur within 4 intervals. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 6/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT This shows the "full" progression of the CC interval. While I did not mention the NC, you can see a White (Open<Close) WRB among the intervals after the SSD. But it does not close above the high of the SSD's Long Shadow. Also note that this CE did not occur in a Zone, but it does create a Zone. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 7/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 8/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community The following user(s) said Thank You: harryniles, Whenever HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #255 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 akifusenet wrote: ...Here is a close up look at the pattern. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 9/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Now comes the rules. I hope somebody else will give the CC rules. And please correct me if I am wrong. (I will give the rules for a bullish signal, reverse the needed parts for a bearish signal) The DSS candle must: (1) have a low lower than the lows of the prior 3 intervals. (2) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle, must be larger than the combination of its Upper shadow and its body. (3) The DSS must appear 1-4 intervals after the appearance of a dark breakout WRB. Therefore, there can be 3 intervals after the dark breakout WRB before we see the DSS. (4) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than the lower shadows of all the intervals after the dark breakout WRB. (5) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than both the lower shadows and the bodies of the 3 intervals Prior to the dark breakout WRB. Akif, The DSS candle must: (1) have a low lower than the lows of the prior three (3) intervals. (2) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle, must be larger than the combination of its Upper Shadow and its body(Open-Close) (3) The DSS must appear 1-4 intervals after the appearance of a Dark BreakOut WRB. Therefore, there can be 3 intervals after the Dark BreakOut WRB before we see the DSS. (4) The Long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than both the lower shadows and the bodies of all the intervals after the Dark BreakOut WRB. (5) The Long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than the lower shadow of the Dark BreakOut WRB. (6) The Long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than both the lower shadows and the bodies of the 3 intervals Prior to the Dark BreakOut WRB. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 11/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The Long Lower Shadow of the DSS interval should be larger than the lower shadows of all intervals involved in the pattern that occur prior to the appearance of the DSS candle. The Long Lower Shadow should also be larger than the bodies (open-close) of all intervals involved in the pattern except the Dark BreakOut WRB. These tell us that the Long Lower Shadow does potentially represent a change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic in the market. Which is why we refer to it as a Demand Swamping Supply (DSS) candle. I have mentioned this before, but it is worth repeating. The CE works best as a visual entry signal. That is to say, when you can look at a chart and visually determine all the rules are present, the signal tends to work better than if you have to do the math to determine if the rules are met. This visual nature is a benefit as one goes down in time frames. The lower the time frame, the less time one has to do the calculations. It also is beneficial if one is trading multiple markets. What signal should be taken? The signal that is visually apparent rather than the signal that needs to be calculated. (2) The long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle, must be larger than the combination of its Upper Shadow and its body(Open-Close) (3) The DSS must appear 1-4 intervals after the appearance of a Dark BreakOut WRB. (4) The Long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than both the lower shadows and the bodies of all the intervals after the Dark BreakOut WRB. (5) The Long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than the lower shadow of the Dark BreakOut WRB. (6) The Long Lower Shadow of the DSS candle must be greater than both the lower shadows and the bodies of the 3 intervals Prior to the Dark BreakOut WRB. (7) The body of the DSS candle must be smaller than at least one of the bodies of the three (3) intervals prior to the Dark BreakOut WRB. (7b) There must be at least one dark body (open>close) amongst the bodies of the three (3) intervals prior to the Dark BreakOut WRB. Note: While the Dark BreakOut WRB changes to White BreakOut WRB for shorts, the requirement of 1 dark interval among the prior three (3) remains dark for shorts. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 34/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community AttachHmOenMtsE: EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: Whenever QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #270 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 At some point in our discovery, we talked about Progression of the start point (now called SSD/DSS). While this is an INVALID signal, it is a great example of progression with the SSD interval. Why is it invalid? It is invalid because there is no dark interval among the three (3) intervals prior to the Dark WRB. I have shaded one of the intervals yellow to simulate a dark interval, so we can think of the pattern as valid. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 35/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 36/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Notice at SSD' we have "valid" signal. Again, it is not actually valid, as there is no dark interval among the three (3) intervals prior to the Dark WRB. But we are pretending that the shaded yellow candle is a dark (close>open) interval. After we see SSD', our thoughts go to finding the CC by the next 4 intervals. While it is not a rule, we would like to see our CC close lower than the close of our SSD' interval. (The actual rule only requires it to close lower than the midpoint of the Long Shadow (LS) of the SSD interval) The next interval turns out to be a SSD interval itself. 1. Its high is greater than the high of the privous three (3) intervals. 2. Its Long Upper Shadow is greater than the combination of its lower shadow and its body. 3. Its Long Upper Shadow is greater than the upper shadows of all the intervals in the pattern, including the upper shadow of the White WRB. 4. Its Long Upper Shadow is greater than the bodies of all intervals in the pattern, excluding the White WRB. 5. Its body is smaller than the body of at least one (1) interval of the three (3) intervals prior to the White WRB. 6. There are no more than three (3) intervals after the appearance of the White WRB and the appearance of the SSD interval. **Note** : this rule does not need to be true for the progression. Only the first SSD interval must meet this requirement. After we see this new SSD interval, we would now begin looking for the CC interval again. Our "count" is set back to zero. Again, notice two intervals later when the CC does appear, it closes lower than the close of the SSD. Also keep in mind that this progression requires the new SSD/DSS intervals to be consecutive, or back to back. Attachments: QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE 41/46 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community THANK YOU EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME HG's Black Sheep Journal #274 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 akifusenet wrote: Hi HG, Amazing additions. CE is evolving very nicely in my opinion. I am just wondering what is the rationale behind the dark body interval requirement? (even for shorts) Akif, Yes, I too think it is evolving nicely. That's a great question about the rationale behind the dark body interval requirement. It is true that the overall concept behind the CE entry could be described as, "a signal pattern manifested by a change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic", with three (3) intervals in particular, the Dark/White WRB, the SSD/DSS , and the CC being necessary elements of that change. The rationale behind the dark body interval is much more utilitarian. In looking at many successful CE pattern signals, I have found the that the vast majority of them have at least one dark interval among the three(3) intervals prior to the Dark/White WRB. This of course does not mean the existence of a dark interval https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 42/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community guarantees a "winning" pattern. Which brings me to the next point. Among those HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT patterns that "fail", I have noticed more of them do not have a dark interval among the three (3) intervals prior to the Dark/White WRB. There is another reason, which I touched on briefly when this project began. Take a look at the chart below. This is one of my favorite types of CE entry. The DSS interval appears immediately after the Dark WRB, and the CC interval appears directly after the DSS interval. The DSS interval also happens to be a Doji. What we also have here is a valid "High Close Doji pattern". One of the requirements of the "High Close Doji Pattern", is a dark interval with its close higher than the close of the Doji, So by simply incorporating the need for a dark interval among the three(3) intervals prior to the Dark WRB, I have increased the possibility that a valid "High Close Doji Pattern" would be picked up. Therefore, instead of looking for both the CE entry and the "High Close Doji Pattern", I can focus on the CE entry. With the knowledge that most valid "High Close Doji Pattern signals will also be CE entry signals. I wanted the CE to be broad enough to encompass a wide range of Price Action and yet be specific enough to incorporate some already known reliable Long Shadow family subgroups. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=250 43/46 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 11 - RTM Community AttachHmOenMtsE: EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: akifusenet QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #275 Posts: 66 Vytautas Offline Trader Thank you received: 89 Hi HG, I see you have market profile on your charts, did you found it useful for your trades? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #279 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=275 5/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 12 - RTM Community Thank you received: 186 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Should we have a contingency plan? To say that the introduction of a contingency plan is a game changer, might be a bit hyperbolic. Yet, the addition of a contingency plan would take us to a new dimension as Price Action traders. So what is a contingency plan? A dictionary definition would be: a course of action to be followed if a preferred plan fails or an existing situation changes. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=275 6/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 12 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT This valid CE (long) leads us to our preferred plan- As we are in a Demand Zone, to enter on the buy side after the appearance of the CC. And then to ride price movement up using successively higher closing WRBs as profit targets, or as trailing stops, or using unfilled opposing Zones as profit targets. There is more to the plan of course, but for now we need only focus on "the riding price up" part. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=275 7/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 12 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Trading is about probabilities, not certainties. So there will be times, perhaps many times, when price does not go up, it goes down. This is why we always want to use a stop. But do we necessarily need our stop to be hit to know that our preferred plan is failing/has failed, or that the existing situation is changing/has changed? Obviously, we do not. So if we can use the available information to tell us we do not want to be long before our stop is hit, can we take that information one step further and deduce that the best course of action is to actually get short? Before we delve into the rules of the contingency plan, we should note a few important things. First, before you can have a valid contingency plan entry, you must have a valid CE. Second, we will be getting short in a Demand Zone and long in a Supply Zone. This is not the same thing as the zones "flipping" (more on this later). It's late. I will finish this soon. I hope your appetite has been peaked............. I did want to note that the Supply Zone is yellow, not because it is part of the contingency plan, but because it is the first WRB Hidden GAP with continuation after the FOMC minutes were released at 2 pm on 10/8. If you're saying to yourself, knowing that makes the likelihood of the contingency plan succeeding even greater, move to the head of the class. You're not just trading patterns, you're trading market context. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=275 8/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 12 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #280 akifusenet Offline Trader Posts: 84 Thank you received: 44 My appetite is up up up... One thing to not on the chart below. A Supply/Demand Delta Zone is not simply created by the appearance of a WRB. The WRB , along with some other requirements,must be a WRB Hidden GAP. Understanding the importance of Hidden GAPs naturally allows us to understand the importance of Actual GAPs. This Actual GAP was created as the new trading week began. We all know the old adage, "Gaps are filled". Well, price shot away from this gap and finally made its way back up to it and filled it. While the gap was filled, the zone created was not closed. Nor was an entry signal necessarily triggered. Yet the zone was an obvious profit target for a long trade taken in the Demand zone below. Chart two is just our basic CE long entry. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=300 13/20 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 13 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Madmoney, cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal Offline Trader #308 Posts: 197 ZeeDeveel 14/20 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=300 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 13 - RTM Community Thank you received: 320 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Hey HG, I can't remember if I thanked you for answering my last question so thanks! I came to RTM via Wyckoff and VSA.. as you may or may not remember from threads elsewhere. Now that I've internalized SD PA trading, I've taken volume off my charts because I found it unnecessary in trading successfully... For various reasons, which, what the heck, let's get into briefly: 1) VSAers get caught up with looking for high volume and miss a lot of trades. The market turns round on low, medium and high volume at different times. 2) NS/ND can be observed in real time by observing the candle being formed, you can see if there is high or low volume by the amount of activity and often the range/spread. When you see a succession of tiny bars, you know the volume was low, especially on a LTF. 3) On a LTF the volume doesn't seem necessary because it can be inferred from the candle with some degree of accuracy.. and on a HTF the tick volume profile tends to be rather flat. 4) If SD/PA is enough, then adding volume risks giving conflicting signals - if it aint broke, why fix it. I'm sure there are other reasons, but those are some thoughts. My question is, for Forex trading, do you think there'd likely be much value in delving back into volume and VSA and trying to supercharge my trading with it, or do you think it would likely be unnecessary and a potential point of distraction and confusion? Cheers man! Cheers man! Hello ZeeDeveel and thank you for the question. I have to be honest, I waffle back and forth on the use of volume all the time now. From an entry signal standpoint, low volume is more important than high volume. Of course those with a deeper understanding of VSA would say, its that low volume occurring where once volume was high that is the real key. If you like how things are going without volume, then there is no reason to add it to the mix. If you do add it, you will miss some trades you would have gotten before. However, the real key is, you will stay out of some bad trades you would have gotten into before. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=300 16/20 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 13 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In fact, many successful traders would argue that the question to ask about any new tool/methodology is not, "What will this get me in to?", but rather, "What will this keep me out of?". EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME Please don't stay quiet my friend. You always bring something to the table with your insights and questions. In a nutshell, the reason the second WRB Hidden Gap does not create a zone is because of cause and effect. Although we don't know that the first WRB is a S/D zone until it meets the strong continuation definition, we do have an idea it will be when we see the second WRB. The reason being the range of the this second WRB is within the range and specifically the body of the first. Therefore, I consider the first to be the origin of the second. Or as two old VSAers might speak, the first Aggression bar is the effort and the second is the result. When there are two consecutive Aggression Bars that are Hidden Gaps, the first one potentially becomes the zone. Here again, the first is the effort and the second is the result. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=25 4/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Basically, we don't want overlapping zones. Therefore if any portion of an Aggression bar is on or within an existing zone, said Aggression bar cannot create its one zone. The zone it is touching/ within is always seen as the true origin of the movement. Just the type of question I would expect from you. Keep 'em coming. My comments are red. LP Hello. I want to answer these questions if I may. First, let's look at the question/comment in the upper right hand corner. There is no Supply/Demand Delta Zone there. Why? Because we do not see any type of strong https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=50 2/47 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 3 - RTM Community continuation before price fills the hidden gap. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The second question/comment is a bit more complex. You are correct that I said the aggression candle should be a WRB using both definitions. Definition 1 defines a WRB as Wide Range Body (open-close) greater then the previous three bodies. This definition is primarily associated with candles. Definition 2 defines a WRB as Wide Range Bar (High-low) greater than the previous three bar's ranges. This definition is primarily associated with bar charts. If you think about it, you can see that it is possible to have an interval meet the definition of a Wide Range Body and not be a Wide Range Bar, and vice versa. By combining the two no matter what type of chart was used, the aggression bars would always be the same. But if one was to choose to only look at candle charts and therefore only focus on the body, it would not be wrong. If one does focus only on the body to define an aggression interval, however, the interval in question should still be on an increasing range. No matter what one chooses, the key is to stay consistant. That was a very nice pick up BTW.. Notice the role the DP (Decision Point) plays in determining if an Aggression interval that might potentially turn into a key change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic and therefore into a Supply/Demand Delta Zone. Take a look at the most recent Climber (Buying Aggression bar). Note how the interval forms a hidden gap that is gapping thru the most recent DP (Decision Point fractal). Now take a look at the Climber in the middle of the chart. Here the Aggression interval does not form a hidden gap thru the most recent up DP. Instead we note how price comes back into the hidden gap but does not fill it and forms a down DP. Another important thing to note is that all climbers shown are meeting the midpoint https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=50 5/47 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 3 - RTM Community trend requirement. That is, the midpoint of their range is greater than or equal to the HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT high of the previous interval. File Attachment: File Name: File Size: 0 KB https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=50 26/47 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Regards OD Hello. This is another case that tells us that we are likely dealing with an aggression bar could be a key change in the Supply/Demand Dynamic. The main thing is how you draw the Supply/Demand Delta Zone. In the chart below you can see two examples. In the one on the left, we see the normal way to draw the Zone. We are drawing the zone from the high and the low of the Aggression interval. The hidden gap is defined as the area between the high of the interval previous to the aggression bar and the low of the interval after the aggression bar. In the example on the right, there is an actual gap that occurs prior to the Aggression bar. If we get the required strong continuation that tells us a key change has occurred, we will draw the Supply/Demand Delta Zone a bit different. We will include the gap as part of the zone. Notice that the low of the zone is not the low of the Aggression bar, but rather the high of the previous interval prior to the actual gap. Basically, we want to include the actual gap in the Zone. Note how the hidden gap is defined as the area from the low of the Aggression bar to the low of the interval proceeding it. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=50 27/47 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Springger, oneday, cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #66 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=50 28/47 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 With all the "excitement", I did not get to post this chart yesterday. In WRB you mark the WRB hidden gap candle as a zone .SD trader will mark base as the zone. As an experience trader, you might have some logic behind using WRB HG method instead of Sam seiden's SD method. Could you kindly shed some light on this. The easy answer is that I found WRB Analysis first and after looking through that prism it's hard to change to Sam's SD method. The harder, and truer, answer has to do with how and why I was attracted to WRB analysis in the first place. I came to WRB analysis after I started my journey into https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 4/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community Volume Spread Analysis (VSA). Tom Williams would talk a lot about looking for a low HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT volume narrow spread (range) interval within the spread(range) of a high volume interval. After looking at numerous charts, I was able to see the validity of his claim. Many good trade set-ups were indeed occurring in the range of a wide spread high volume interval. As I was seeing this phenomenon, I came across a post by Mark where he mentioned WRBs. While Mark did not use VSA, I immediately thought that there must be some connection. Or at the very least, they would be compatible. Despite the fact that Mark was originally dealing with the body of a candle and VSA does not look at the open, WRB (Wide Range Body) analysis proved to be very usable with VSA. By the time I was introduced to Sam's work, I was already a firm believer in the power of the concept of a WRB hidden gap. I also have a problem with one aspect of Sam's method highlighted by your second picture. I do not believe that a Supply/Demand Zone is a trade signal in and of itself. Nor do I believe that Supply/Demand Zones can change polarity. Actually, I should say that in all the videos I have watched, Sam never mentions that zones can reverse polarity. Yet in all the threads I read about SD, the concept of supply becoming demand and vice versa is used. I don't think this is accurate. If a demand zone is created by a rally base rally pattern and a supply zone is created by a drop base drop pattern, then the only way a demand zone can become a supply zone is if there is a drop base drop at the exact same place. In other words, merely cutting through a demand zone and then finding support (a totally different concept than supply and demand) does not in my opinion constitute a change in polarity. I like the fact that all zones close in WRB analysis. There is no concept of reversing polarity. Which is really a concept of support and resistance. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #79 Posts: 143 HiddenGap Offline Trader Thank you received: 186 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 6/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community Les_HPOauMl wErote: DeEaDr UHCGA, TION FORUM MORE LOGOUT could you pls explain the Fakie setup when u have time? Thank you! LP Hello my friend. The Fakie (or Fakey) is a price action pattern that comes from Nial Fuller's site. There are a few FREE videos on his site or youtube showing it with a bit more detail than follows. Basically, the Fakie (or Fakey) is a price action pattern that requires there to be a false- break of an inside bar setup. So, once you have an inside bar setup, you can watch for a false-break of the inside bar and the mother bar. Take a look at the chart below. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 7/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT A. Is called the Mother candle. We only know it is the Mother candle after we see B. B. Inside interval. That means this interval does not make a higher high or a lower low than the previous candle. C. This interval makes a false break of the high of both the inside bar and the Mother candle, but it closes back within the range of the Mother candle. Note that the intervals between X and Y also form a fakie. However, this would never be taken because no part of any interval in the pattern is occurring within a valid Supply/Demand Zone. The false break must be on the first interval that trades outside the range of the Mother candle. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 8/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Les_Paul, changbachang, chiron, blacky, PASniper, akifusenet, Whenever QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 9/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community THANK YOU EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME HG's Black Sheep Journal #80 changbachang Offline Trader Be Patient, Be Modest, Be Good Posts: 471 Thank you received: 281 HiddenGap wrote: Basically, the Fakie (or Fakey) is a price action pattern that requires there to be a false-break of an inside bar setup. The easy answer is that I found WRB Analysis rst and after looking through that prism it's hard to change to Sam's SD method. The harder, and truer, answer has to do with how and why I was attracted to WRB analysis in the rst place. I came to WRB analysis after I started my journey into https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 21/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community Volume Spread Analysis (VSA). Tom Williams would talk a lot about looking for a HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT low volume narrow spread (range) interval within the spread(range) of a high volume interval. After looking at numerous charts, I was able to see the validity of his claim. Many good trade set-ups were indeed occurring in the range of a wide spread high volume interval. As I was seeing this phenomenon, I came across a post by Mark where he mentioned WRBs. While Mark did not use VSA, I immediately thought that there must be some connection. Or at the very least, they would be compatible. Despite the fact that Mark was originally dealing with the body of a candle and VSA does not look at the open, WRB (Wide Range Body) analysis proved to be very usable with VSA. By the time I was introduced to Sam's work, I was already a rm believer in the power of the concept of a WRB hidden gap. I also have a problem with one aspect of Sam's method highlighted by your second picture. I do not believe that a Supply/Demand Zone is a trade signal in and of itself. Nor do I believe that Supply/Demand Zones can change polarity. Actually, I should say that in all the videos I have watched, Sam never mentions that zones can reverse polarity. Yet in all the threads I read about SD, the concept of supply becoming demand and vice versa is used. I don't think this is accurate. If a demand zone is created by a rally base rally pattern and a supply zone is created by a drop base drop pattern, then the only way a demand zone can become a supply zone is if there is a drop base drop at the exact same place. In other words, merely cutting through a demand zone and then nding support (a totally di erent concept than supply and demand) does not in my opinion constitute a change in polarity. I like the fact that all zones close in WRB analysis. There is no concept of reversing polarity. Which is really a concept of support and resistance. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #99 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 38/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 gristar wrote: Hi HG! good day to u. Other than fakie and engulfing, what is other entry signal you use? Do we enter whenever we see engulfing or fakie in hidden gap? We do not care if the entry signal happen at top or bottom part of the hidden gap if the hidden gap is very wide range? Can you show some example when a open hidden gap become closed zone. How aboutstop loss placement,is it at the end of fakie or end of delta zone? Hope to hear from you soon. Thanks in advance. Hello Gristar. Lots of questions there. Awesome! I want to first focus on the issue of a zone closing. Take a look at the chart. There are three (3) possible ways a zone can be considered closed. That is to say, each individual can choose which method he or she prefers and then stick to that one for consistency. Methods #1. One can consider the zone closed, after price action has filled the zone. #2. One can consider the zone closed, after price action closes above the zone (for a Supply Zone). #3. One can consider the zone closed, after a full candle body (white for a Supply Zone) is formed above the the Zone. This body should not be a doji. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=75 39/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I prefer #3. If the interval in question is involved in an entry signal pattern, the zone would remain open. For example, #3 on this chart could have turned out to be part of a valid Fakie pattern. If it did turn out to be a valid Fakie Pattern, then the trade would be taken and the zone would be considered still open. Also note that one can take trades anywhere within the zone. In fact, there really only needs to be on tick/pip of an entry signal touching some part of the zone for the signal to be considered. So if there are 5 candles in your entry signal pattern, only one tick or pip of one of the intervals has to be touching the zone. I will get into the rules on the next post. I just wanted to show this, and say thanks for the question. I'll wet your appetite with this, notice that I have used the term "SnapBack". This is because there is a direct relationship between this candle line and the candle lines used that are called "Climbers" and "Drifters"....... Take a look at the chart below: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=100 9/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT A down Snapback (as in the example above) starts out with the characteristics of a Drifter and then reveres course as demand swamps the supply. One requirement for a Drifter is that its close be lower than the lows of the previous three intervals. This rule makes the Drifter a "Brake Out" interval. For the Snapback, we see that the Low of the interval is low enough so that if the interval closed on it, it would meet that "Break Out" requirement. Whilst we use WRB to mean Wide Range Body when talking about Drifter or Climbers, we use WRB to mean Wide Range Bar(or candle) when defining the SnapBack. This is an implication that the Snapback interval was a Drifter at sometime during its construction. So, since it is reaction of price action, we focus on price action. I don't know how masters trade here but this is how I trade. 1] I look for a good 1H, 4H, D1 S/D. 2] I look how price approaching to these zones. 3] I look if it is QM level ? or is there FTR at left ? or is there RBD/DBR left ? Or is there any decision to engulf left ? If yes then it is good. 4] When price is near to these zones, I go to the LTF and I look for Compression to confirm if orders getting consumed. (For ex. If price is approaching the supply zone, I will look for compression at the end of the swing while it is reaching to the supply zone. And if i see compression, it can be confirmed that buy orders are getting consumed. See the video of compression) (If i see no compression in LTF then I usually don't go for sell.) 5] When I see compression, if i see a new RBD/ QM into the LTF then I go for sell. 6] When I go for sell, I look for any untouched/unconsumed demand to the downside. (Because price can retrace there again to the upside) 7] And if there is any unconsumed demand, then I look the approach of price to that demand as point no. 2. Look IF"s all videos and read article on the site again and again. Read the journals of Masters. You would get an idea how, when and why we enter trade and what we look for enter trade. Experts please correct me if I am wrong. That's correct, I draw the lines manually. 29/37 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=100 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT akifusenet wrote: ..I programmed your Agression bar definition and it gives a lot of signals. It looks like you choose the best looking ones manually.Or am I wrong? So the question is if choose them manually what is your criteria? How do you choose them? Akif. We first need to define an Aggression on bar. All Aggression bars will represent changes in the Supply/Demand dynamic in the market. However, not all Aggression bars will represent KEY changes in the Supply/Demand dynamic in the market. Only those that represent KEY changes will be used to determine a Supply/Demand Delta Zone. One of the most important requirements for a Aggression bar, is that it forms a Hidden Gap. This particular element is not part of my " Aggression bar finder indicator", so you will see an indication of an Aggression bar on some bars that do not form Hidden Gaps. In reality, they are not Aggression bars and are immediately disregarded. The other elements that Constitute and Aggression bar are as follows: 1. For a Climber, the open is in the lower 25% of the interval's range, while the close is in the upper 75% of the interval's range. For a Drifter, the open is in the upper 75% of the interval's range, and the close is in the lower 25% of the interval's range. 2. The range of the interval (open -close) is greater than the previous interval's range. 3. Volume is increasing. 4. The interval is a Wide Range Body (WRB). https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=100 30/37 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community 5. The midpoint of the range is greater than the high of the previous range for a HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Climber. The midpoint of the range is less than the low of the previous interval for a Drifter. 6. For a Climber, the low of the interval is greater than or equal to the low of the previous interval. That makes it a "Buying bar". For a Drifter, the high of the interval is less than or equal to the high of the previous interval. That makes it a "Selling bar". In other words, a Climber will make a higher high but not a lower low than the previous interval. And a Drifter will make a lower low and not a higher high than the previous interval. Once you have an Aggression bar, you have a change in the Supply/Demand dynamic in the market. To determine if it is a KEY change, we look at how the market reacts to it. We look for strong continuation to verify that a change has taken place. FORUM HOME EDUCATION MORE LOGOUT Akif, Hello Akifusenet, Zones do not overlap and we don't use zones within zones. Once you have an Aggression interval that creates a Hidden Gap, you look for strong continuation. For example, after a Climber, you want to see two consecutive UP intervals that are closing higher, making higher highs and lower lowers prior to the next swing. If the potential Zone area is closed before you see strong continuation, it is not a Zone. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT At this point, we have a maximum of 3 intervals for price to retrace back into the wick of our "stop run candle". The idea being, that we want to place a stop a few pips below the low of the "stop run candle". Therefore, we would like to minimize our risk by entering as low as possible. One can choose to wait for a 50% retracement of that lower wick to enter. In this case that did not happen. But let's assume a more aggressive style. So we are simply waiting for a retrace that is lower than the low of candle #2 and back into the lower wick of candle #1. We get this at #4. Now we are in the market. Our risk is defined as the shaded area. Ultimately, we take a loss on this trade. But take a look at the yellow arrow. The arrow points to a down WRB that closes lower then the low of the candle that triggered us into the market. This could have served as an early warning that the trade was not going to work. So an early exit could be contemplated at this time. One key here is that the WRB occurs within 1-5 intervals after the trigger bar. Yes, they need the retrace to be able to get into a trade with no https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=125 37/50 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community more than 20 tick stop, and it's a system that works, only thing that I'm saying is make HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT sure the retrace confirms as a test in the direction of the trade, with volume etc. Or not, I may be over analyzing it all, that's why they have these rules, so they don't have to care or think about anything I'm talking about. There are a few absolute truths about the market. One is as soon as you buy, you become a seller. And as soon as you sell, you become a buyer. Another truth is that the market moves more because of traders trying to exit losing positions, than it does because of traders entering new ones. Statement one is very obvious and very true. Statement two I have of course heard from Drager, but I personally don't believe this is always true, 50% at most. The following user(s) said Thank You: hieronymus QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #146 Posts: 143 HiddenGap Offline Trader 41/50 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=125 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community Thank you received: 186 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Here's an example of the flexibility this entry "pattern" can allow. What I want to create is a rule based entry that is specific in nature, yet general enough to allow for a wide range of possibilities. We begin with the appearance of a WRB. There are a couple of ways to do this. We wait for the appearance of the WRB and then focus on the next 1-3 intervals for our Start Point Candle. Or, we can identify the Start Point Candle first, and look back 1-3 intervals to see if there is a WRB. I think it is easier to identify the WRB and see it as an alert that a signal might be coming. 1': This is our Start Point. It is making a higher high than the previous candle and its upper shadow is greater than the combination of its lower shadow and its body. 2': This candle does not confirm. It does not close lower than the body of the previous candle (the Start Point Candle) and its body is not large than the body of the previous candle. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=125 42/50 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community At this point there is no trade signal. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT But wait. The next candle makes a higher high than the previous candle and has an upper shadow greater than the combination of its lower shadow and body. And it is within 1-3 intervals of the WRB. We have a valid Start Point Candle. A new #1. 2: This candle does close lower than the body of the Start Point Candle and also has a body greater than the body of the Start Point Candle. 3: We can look to enter as price retraces into the area of the Upper Shadow of our Start Point Candle. So this is a "Valid" signal, but it does not appear in a Zone. Take my chart on post #140 as an example. Just imagine that the candle after #2 wasn't a doji and was at least a 1 tick green candle. That would follow all the rules then and then candle #3 would be a perfect pullback. This seems to happen a lot, where it takes 2 candles to close above/below the SP candle. . There is indeed a Progression of Candles in this pattern, at least with respect to our Start Point. In this schematic, I have drawn what a Progression of the Start Point can look like. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=150 2/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT After the WRB, we have our first Start Point. A Start Point must make a higher high than the previous interval and the upper shadow must be larger than the combination of its lower shadow and its body. This is the candle we start our count on. The next candle should confirm the possible shift in the Supply/Demand dynamic that our Start Candle represents. In this case, the next candle does not confirm 1A. But it is by definition another Start Point. So we begin our count anew. We can continue to restart our count as long as we are getting consecutive Start Points. Note at 1D we are now beyond 3 intervals after a WRB. Since the initial Start Point was within the 1-3 interval requirement, we are able to continue. In this example, 2 is our confirmation candle. The confirmation candle must close lower than the body of the previous candle and it must have a large body than the body of the previous candle. Suppose that 2 did not appear, but we got 1F instead. This would be the LAST time we could restart our count. Typically a Zone closes with a close above/below unless the interval that closes above/below is part of a entry signal pattern. In this case not only does this interval close above the zone, but its entire range is above the zone. So the very next candle would have to be a confirmation candle or we would not have the possibility of an entry. Thank you for joining in the discussion, From the standpoint of our stop, we would want to use the lower low +/- x amount of pips (this would be a personal choice so I am not giving an exact number). So in this case, the stop would be placed below the confirmation candle's low. This is a very interesting case you show. Based on the rules, #2 is a confirmation candle of #1. It is also a candle that is BOTH a Start Point and a Confirmation candle. In other words, this candle is a #1 and #2 at the same time. In general, we want to see price retrace into the shadow of the Start Point. As this represents the area where the change in the Supply/Demand dynamic has likely occurred. If the confirmation candle makes a lower low, then we would want to see the retracement go deeper into the shadow of our Start Point. If you take the difference between the lows and require a pull back of equal amount. That is, suppose the difference between the low of #1 and #2 is 10 pips. To enter not only do we need to see a retracement into the shadow, but we need to see that retracement be 10 pips deep. Rule: The lower shadow (for longs) of our Start Point must be greater than the lower shadows and the bodies of the 3 intervals prior to the WRB. If there is a WRB among the 3 intervals, we use 3 intervals prior to that WRB. Note: The lower shadow of our Start Point must be larger than the combination of its upper shadow and its body. For the 3 intervals prior to the WRB, the lower shadow has to be larger than both the lower shadows and the bodies separately, not combined. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=150 40/44 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT This is a Yen 60 min. chart.
