However, we do have a HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Start Point and are new rule is in place. Let's see what happens.... Update on the Yen. This one poses more questions than it gives answers. Ultimately, this one gets into trade management. Remember that the reason we want to see an retracement, is so that we can minimize our risk. We want to place our stop x pips/ticks below the low of the Stop Run (SR) candle. Therefore, the lower price pulls back into the lower shadow, the less our risk will be. Simply put, a pull back into the lower shadow is needed to validate the signal. How much of a pull back into the lower shadow is based on an individual's risk tolerance. One question that arises is, are we only going to exit with a loss if our stop is hit? If we are only getting out when our stop is hit, we could actually still be long at this moment. Depending on pro t targets and position size of course. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=175 11/34 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Which brings up another issue. As previously stated, we can minimize risk by HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT requiring greater retracements into the lower shadow. Another option is to wait for less of a pull back, but to decrease position size instead. Now we are starting to get into trade management at this point. So let's come back to this once we have establish what constitutes a valid entry signal............ Attachments: QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #184 Posts: 143 HiddenGap Offline Trader 12/34 Thank you received: 186 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=175 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Here's aHcOoMupEle of exaEmDpUleCsAfrToImONthe Euro.FORUM MORE LOGOUT Let's start with the long (numbered in black). Updated rules: Our count begins with the appearance of a Start Point (SP) candle. The Start Point candle must: (1) have a low lower than the lows of the prior 3 intervals. (2) The long Lower Shadow of the Start Point (SP) candle, must be larger than the combination of its Upper shadow and its body. (3) The Start Point must appear 1-3 intervals after the appearance of a WRB. (4) The long Lower Shadow of the SP candle must be larger than both the lower shadows and the bodies of the 3 intervals Prior to the WRB. This is our #1 The Confirmation Candle (CC) must: (1) close lower than the body of the prior candle. (2) have a body larger than the body of the prior candle. In our 5 candle sequence, the CC must be #1, 2,3, or 4. After we have a Confirmation Candle, price must pull back within the Lower Long Shadow of the Start Candle to validate the sequence and trigger entry. The red count is for a short trade. This count includes the progression of candles. 13/34 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=175 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Notice that the CC candle is #3, not #2. This trade would not have been taken as we HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT are in a Supply/Demand Delta Zone. Attachments: QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 5/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HG's Black Sheep Journal #203 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 I believe we can call the modifications set. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 6/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The new modifications are: A. There must be a WRB in the sequence. This isn't so much a new rule as it is making the appearance of a WRB definitive. B. The initial Start Point (SP) must appear 1-4 intervals after the WRB. We've gone from 1-3 to 1-4. Therefore, there can be 3 intervals after the WRB before we see the SP. C. The Long Lower Shadow (for longs) of the initial Start Point (SP) must be greater than the lower shadows of all the intervals after the WRB and the 3 intervals prior to the WRB. And the Long Lower Shadow must be larger than the bodies of said intervals as well. But the Long Lower Shadow does not have to be larger than the combination of the lower shadow and the body of any interval except its own. If we care then that bearish SP woul be invalid. I know that CC is missing in this picture but the question is about the rules for identifying the SP candles. Akif, Great questions, keep them coming. I will start with this one first. One of our goals is to create an entry signal that is specific and rule-based, yet broad enough to cover a myriad of situations. To that end, we have not mentioned any type of color (close vs open) requirements for any of the intervals. I have noticed that the majority (almost all) of the valid short signals will have a White (close>open) WRB. And the valid long signals will have Dark (close<open) WRB. But again, that is not a requirement, only an observation. Along those same lines, we have not made any such requirement for any of the intervals involved in the pattern. For example, we have NOT said that there must be a Dark (close>open) interval within the 3 intervals prior to the WRB. Once again, great question. I appreciate your participation. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 12/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #209 HiddenGap Offline Trader Posts: 143 Thank you received: 186 [quote="akifusenet" post=43700]Another trick question is: What is there are consecutive WRBs before the SP? which WRB's prev 3 candles will be looked at? One thing I have avoided discussing is the idea of a "contingency" plan. What I mean by "contingency" plan is price action that would not only tell us we are wrong, but would also suggest we take a trade in the opposite direction. I do not like "contingency" plan set-ups, because they would have us going long in Supply Zones and short in Demand Zones. However, we may still want to focus on a particular Price Action piece typically found in a "contingency" plan. The WRB. I will get into the concept on the next post. For now, just realize that we also have a Nullification Candle (NC). The white (open<close) WRB in this picture, although we are not happy to see it, would not be a NC. One thing I should mention about placing our stops. I have been saying the stop should be roughly 5 pips from the high/low of the SP. I have been using 5 because that is what DTML use. But they are taking the trades on a 15 minute chart. 5 pips may or may not be appropriate on a 1 hour chart. They are also trying to keep the over risk to 20 pips. Again, 20 pips may or may not be appropriate on a 1 hour time frame. Akif, While there will always be losing trades, let's take a closer look at these here. A1: I am not sure that this is in fact a valid SP. The long lower shadow does not look larger than the body of the candle before it. Nor does it look larger than the lower shadow of the candle before it. The Confirmation Entry is a VISUAL ENTRY. That means the better set-ups tend to be those that you can tell meet the requirements visually with ease. Now, you may do the actual math and conclude that lower shadow is larger than the body of the previous candle. But when you can make that determination easily with the naked eye, the set-up tends to be better. B1: This is more of an obvious valid SP. A valid signal is produced, which results in a loss. Let's take a closer look at B5. This is a White (open<close) WRB that appears 1-3 intervals after our entry candle and closes higher than the high of the entry candle. That is a Nullification Candle (NC). In this case, the NC stops us out. But if it had not stopped us out, we would want to be exiting, or at the very least, moving our stop closer to our entry price. A true "contingency" plan would have us stop and reverse our position. Especially since the WRB preceded a doji candle line. The other place where an NC can appear is after the SP but before confirmation. This NC would tell us not to enter the trade at all. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 29/38 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Note that our NC could be the interval that both enters us into the trade during its creation and tells us to exit once it is complete. Which is the case here. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU HG's Black Sheep Journal #221 Posts: 143 HiddenGap Offline Trader Thank you received: 186 Here's a schematic with a few examples of the NC. Key points: 30/38 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1917-hg-s-black-sheep-journal?start=200 7/27/2018 HG's Black Sheep Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT 1. Any white (open<close) WRB that closes higher than the high of our SP is a NC. If the NC appears before the CC, we would not enter at all. 2. If we are in a short trade, a white (open<close) WRB 1-3 intervals after our entry candle that closes higher than the high of said candle, is a NC. I missed that one. Pt 4 should be where you marked it. Pt 5 would remain the same. I am using successively higher closing WRBs. So Pt 3 is not the first WRB after Pt 2. It's the first WRB after Pt 2 that closes higher. Pt 1 is a little more hazy. I like to see it be x amount away from the entry and/or clear x amount of the range of the signal pattern. That is why Pt 1 is not the WRB 2 intervals after 4.
